//! Does a **derived** key actually work with the library the ledger uses?
//!
//! `CLAUDE.md`: *"When keygen is written, validate it against mpt-crypto's own
//! test vectors — `test_pok_sk`, `test_elgamal`, `test_elgamal_verify` — before
//! trusting it."* Those three are C programs over **randomly generated** keys,
//! so they cannot be run against ours as they stand. What is portable is the
//! set of properties they establish, driven here with a derived key in place of
//! the random one:
//!
//! | upstream test | property | here |
//! |---|---|---|
//! | `test_elgamal` | keypair generates, encrypts, and decrypts back | [`a_derived_key_encrypts_and_decrypts_through_the_library`] |
//! | `test_elgamal_verify` | ciphertexts verify against a revealed amount | [`a_derived_key_passes_the_libraries_revealed_amount_check`] |
//! | `test_pok_sk` | a registration proof of knowledge verifies, and its negatives are rejected | [`a_derived_key_produces_a_registration_proof_the_library_accepts`] |
//!
//! # ⚠ And the sentence that matters more than any of them
//!
//! The same `CLAUDE.md` paragraph continues: *"**Necessary, not sufficient:**
//! those establish that a keypair is well-formed and interoperates, and a
//! constant `sk_A = 1` passes all three."* That is a claim, so it gets a test
//! that would fail if it were false —
//! [`a_constant_sk_of_one_passes_every_upstream_check`] runs `sk_A = 1` through
//! all three and asserts they **pass**, which is what makes
//! `tests/keygen_kat.rs` load-bearing rather than decorative.
//!
//! Requires the vendored patched library. `tools/rebuild-env.sh`.

use wconf_signer::keygen;
use wconf_signer::prover::{self, ElGamalKeypair, Participant};
use wconf_signer::scalar::{reduce_wide, Scalar};

/// The keygen ruling's Vector 1.
const V1_SEED: &str = "sp6JdwovBCsiwnMhXuvZGZtPUoGVj";
/// The keygen ruling's Vector 2 — an `sEd…` seed, which is what every account
/// this project holds on Devnet actually is.
const V2_SEED: &str = "sEdSJHdnVumf99WfaHTnU8DaQkx5Q4n";

const ISSUANCE: [u8; 24] = [
    0x00, 0x46, 0x25, 0xC9, 0xF2, 0xDD, 0xC3, 0xE6, 0x52, 0x07, 0x1A, 0x9F, 0xFA, 0x07, 0x50, 0x7F,
    0x21, 0x51, 0xFE, 0xE3, 0x32, 0xCB, 0x25, 0xE8,
];

fn blinding(tag: u8) -> Scalar {
    let mut wide = [0u8; 64];
    wide[63] = tag;
    wide[0] = 0x11;
    reduce_wide(&wide).expect("a fixed non-zero test blinding factor")
}

/// `test_elgamal`'s round trip, with a derived key.
#[test]
fn a_derived_key_encrypts_and_decrypts_through_the_library() {
    for seed in [V1_SEED, V2_SEED] {
        let kp = keygen::derive_from_master_seed(seed, None).expect(seed).into_keypair();
        let r = blinding(7);
        for amount in [0u64, 1, 1001, 65_535] {
            let ct = prover::encrypt(amount, kp.public(), &r)
                .unwrap_or_else(|e| panic!("{seed}: encrypting {amount}: {e}"));
            let got = prover::decrypt(&ct, kp.secret(), 0, 70_000)
                .unwrap_or_else(|e| panic!("{seed}: decrypting {amount}: {e}"));
            assert_eq!(got, amount, "{seed}: the round trip changed the amount");
        }

        // The negative that stops the positives being vacuous: another key's
        // secret must NOT decrypt it.
        let other = keygen::derive_from_master_seed(V2_SEED, None).unwrap().into_keypair();
        if other.public() != kp.public() {
            let ct = prover::encrypt(4242, kp.public(), &r).unwrap();
            assert!(
                prover::decrypt(&ct, other.secret(), 0, 10_000).is_err(),
                "{seed}: a different key decrypted this ciphertext"
            );
        }
    }
}

/// `test_elgamal_verify`'s check, with a derived key on both sides of a
/// two-participant set.
#[test]
fn a_derived_key_passes_the_libraries_revealed_amount_check() {
    let holder_kp = keygen::derive_from_master_seed(V1_SEED, None).unwrap().into_keypair();
    let issuer_kp = ElGamalKeypair::generate().expect("the library's own generator");
    let r = blinding(9);
    let amount = 500u64;

    let holder = Participant {
        pubkey: *holder_kp.public(),
        ciphertext: prover::encrypt(amount, holder_kp.public(), &r).unwrap(),
    };
    let issuer = Participant {
        pubkey: *issuer_kp.public(),
        ciphertext: prover::encrypt(amount, issuer_kp.public(), &r).unwrap(),
    };

    assert!(
        prover::verify_revealed_amount(amount, &r, &holder, &issuer, None),
        "the library rejected ciphertexts it produced from a derived key"
    );

    // A verifier that says OK to everything makes every other assertion here
    // vacuous — `REVIEW-009` §8 found exactly that in `t-nbound.c`.
    assert!(
        !prover::verify_revealed_amount(amount + 1, &r, &holder, &issuer, None),
        "the revealed-amount check accepted the wrong amount"
    );
    assert!(
        !prover::verify_revealed_amount(amount, &blinding(10), &holder, &issuer, None),
        "the revealed-amount check accepted the wrong blinding factor"
    );
}

/// `test_pok_sk`, with a derived key and the **account-bound** context this
/// signer always supplies.
#[test]
fn a_derived_key_produces_a_registration_proof_the_library_accepts() {
    let key = keygen::derive_from_master_seed(V1_SEED, None).unwrap();
    let account = *key.account_id();
    let kp = key.into_keypair();

    let context = prover::convert_context_hash(&account, &ISSUANCE, 42).unwrap();
    let proof = prover::convert_proof(kp.public(), kp.secret(), &context)
        .expect("the library refused a derived key's registration proof");

    assert!(
        prover::verify_convert_proof(&proof, kp.public(), &context),
        "the library rejected its own registration proof"
    );

    // Upstream's own three negatives.
    let mut wrong_context = context;
    wrong_context[0] ^= 0xFF;
    assert!(
        !prover::verify_convert_proof(&proof, kp.public(), &wrong_context),
        "the registration proof verified under the wrong context"
    );

    let mut corrupted = proof;
    corrupted[63] ^= 0x01;
    assert!(
        !prover::verify_convert_proof(&corrupted, kp.public(), &context),
        "a corrupted registration proof verified"
    );

    let other = ElGamalKeypair::generate().unwrap();
    assert!(
        !prover::verify_convert_proof(&proof, other.public(), &context),
        "the registration proof verified under the wrong public key"
    );
}

/// `CLAIMS.md` **F-19**, as a property rather than a note: the registration
/// proof this signer emits is **bound to the account it is for**.
///
/// The library accepts a NULL `context_id` and then emits an account-unbound,
/// freely replayable registration proof. rippled cannot reach that path; this
/// signer links the library directly and could. Ruling §5 item 4 says to assert
/// non-NULL at the call site — here that assertion is a **type**:
/// [`prover::convert_proof`] takes `&[u8; 32]`, which cannot be null in safe
/// Rust, and `mpt::secp256k1_mpt_pok_sk_prove` is declared but never re-exported
/// through `prover`.
///
/// What is testable is the consequence, and this is it: a proof made for one
/// account's context does not verify for another's. If the context ever stopped
/// binding the account — a NULL, a constant, a hash that forgot to include the
/// AccountID — this test fails.
#[test]
fn the_registration_proof_does_not_transfer_to_another_account() {
    let a = keygen::derive_from_master_seed(V1_SEED, None).unwrap();
    let b = keygen::derive_from_master_seed(V2_SEED, None).unwrap();
    let (acct_a, acct_b) = (*a.account_id(), *b.account_id());
    assert_ne!(acct_a, acct_b, "the two vectors must be different accounts");

    let kp = a.into_keypair();
    let ctx_a = prover::convert_context_hash(&acct_a, &ISSUANCE, 1).unwrap();
    let ctx_b = prover::convert_context_hash(&acct_b, &ISSUANCE, 1).unwrap();
    assert_ne!(ctx_a, ctx_b, "two accounts produced the same convert context hash");

    let proof = prover::convert_proof(kp.public(), kp.secret(), &ctx_a).unwrap();
    assert!(prover::verify_convert_proof(&proof, kp.public(), &ctx_a), "the control failed");
    assert!(
        !prover::verify_convert_proof(&proof, kp.public(), &ctx_b),
        "a registration proof made for one account verified for another — this is F-19's \
         harm, and it is what a NULL context_id produces"
    );

    // The same account at a different sequence is also a different context, so
    // a registration proof cannot be lifted from one transaction into another.
    let ctx_a2 = prover::convert_context_hash(&acct_a, &ISSUANCE, 2).unwrap();
    assert!(
        !prover::verify_convert_proof(&proof, kp.public(), &ctx_a2),
        "a registration proof verified at a different sequence"
    );
}

/// ⚠ **The control that makes `tests/keygen_kat.rs` necessary.**
///
/// `sk_A = 1` is the degenerate key: its public key is the generator itself,
/// and anyone can decrypt everything encrypted to it. It is also a perfectly
/// well-formed secp256k1 keypair, so **every check in this file passes for
/// it**. That is asserted here rather than asserted about, because
/// "necessary but not sufficient" is a claim and this project's rule is that a
/// claim needs a test that fails if it is false.
///
/// The consequence: interop tests say nothing about whether a key was derived
/// correctly. Only the known-answer vectors do.
#[test]
fn a_constant_sk_of_one_passes_every_upstream_check() {
    use k256::elliptic_curve::sec1::ToEncodedPoint;
    let mut sk = [0u8; 32];
    sk[31] = 1;
    // Computed, not recalled. `CLAIMS.md` E-95: an expected value written from
    // recollection is the exact defect this project has already shipped once.
    let pk_point = k256::SecretKey::from_slice(&sk).unwrap().public_key().to_encoded_point(true);
    let mut pk = [0u8; 33];
    pk.copy_from_slice(pk_point.as_bytes());
    let degenerate = ElGamalKeypair::try_from_parts(sk, pk).expect("sk = 1 is a valid keypair");

    // test_elgamal — round trip.
    let r = blinding(3);
    let ct = prover::encrypt(777, degenerate.public(), &r).expect("encrypt under sk = 1");
    assert_eq!(
        prover::decrypt(&ct, degenerate.secret(), 0, 1000).expect("decrypt under sk = 1"),
        777,
        "sk = 1 failed the round trip, so this control proves nothing"
    );

    // test_elgamal_verify — revealed amount.
    let issuer = ElGamalKeypair::generate().unwrap();
    let holder_p = Participant { pubkey: pk, ciphertext: ct };
    let issuer_p = Participant {
        pubkey: *issuer.public(),
        ciphertext: prover::encrypt(777, issuer.public(), &r).unwrap(),
    };
    assert!(
        prover::verify_revealed_amount(777, &r, &holder_p, &issuer_p, None),
        "sk = 1 failed the revealed-amount check"
    );

    // test_pok_sk — registration proof.
    let context = prover::convert_context_hash(&[9u8; 20], &ISSUANCE, 1).unwrap();
    let proof = prover::convert_proof(&pk, &sk, &context).expect("PoK under sk = 1");
    assert!(
        prover::verify_convert_proof(&proof, &pk, &context),
        "sk = 1 failed the registration proof"
    );

    // Every one of those passed. And it is not the derived key:
    let derived = keygen::derive_from_master_seed(V1_SEED, None).unwrap();
    assert_ne!(
        derived.public(),
        &pk,
        "the derivation produced sk = 1, which would make the control meaningless"
    );
}

/// A derived key must survive the trip through the profile loader, which
/// recomputes `sk·G` and refuses a pair that does not correspond.
#[test]
fn a_derived_key_is_accepted_by_the_keypair_validator_and_a_mismatched_one_is_not() {
    let key = keygen::derive_from_master_seed(V1_SEED, None).unwrap();
    let pk = *key.public();
    let kp = key.into_keypair();
    assert_eq!(kp.public(), &pk);

    // Swap in another key's public half — the failure mode that permanently
    // loses a balance, because everything downstream succeeds.
    let other = *ElGamalKeypair::generate().unwrap().public();
    let err = ElGamalKeypair::try_from_parts(*kp.secret(), other)
        .expect_err("a mismatched keypair was accepted");
    assert!(
        err.reason.contains("not sk·G"),
        "the refusal does not say what is wrong: {}",
        err.reason
    );
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Two REVIEW-020 findings that were wrong, pinned by tests rather than by
// disposition notes (`CLAIMS.md` E-93, E-106)
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// ⚠ **A rejected review claim.** `REVIEW-020`'s Codex leg reported, as its only
/// HIGH, that `DerivedKey::into_keypair` *"cannot compile"* — because it reads
/// `self.sk.0` out of a `Zeroizing<32>`, and Rust forbids moving a field out of
/// a type implementing `Drop`. Its stated consequence was that *"the new derived
/// key path cannot ship."*
///
/// The premise is false. `[u8; 32]` is `Copy`, so `self.sk.0` is a **copy**, not
/// a move, and the restriction does not apply. Every build of this crate is a
/// counterexample.
///
/// But the worry underneath it is real and is worth a test rather than a note:
/// *does the secret survive its source being zeroized?* `self` is dropped at the
/// end of `into_keypair`, which overwrites the original buffer. If the handed-out
/// value were an alias rather than a copy, the keypair would leave that function
/// holding thirty-two zero bytes.
///
/// ⚠ **And `try_from_parts` would not catch that**, though not for the reason an
/// earlier version of this comment gave. It *does* validate — it recomputes
/// `sk·G` — but it runs while the source is still alive, so an aliased buffer
/// validates correctly and is wiped afterwards, on the way out. The check is
/// synchronous; the zeroization is not. `REVIEW-020` round two caught the
/// mis-description.
#[test]
fn the_derived_secret_survives_its_source_being_zeroized() {
    let key = keygen::derive_from_master_seed(V1_SEED, None).unwrap();
    let pk = *key.public();

    // `key` is consumed and its `Zeroizing<32>` runs its destructor here.
    let kp = key.into_keypair();

    assert_ne!(kp.secret(), &[0u8; 32], "the secret was zeroized on the way out");
    assert_eq!(kp.public(), &pk, "the public key changed across into_keypair");

    // The property that actually matters: the secret still decrypts what the
    // public key encrypts. A zeroed or truncated copy fails here.
    let r = blinding(11);
    let ct = prover::encrypt(31337, kp.public(), &r).expect("encrypt");
    assert_eq!(
        prover::decrypt(&ct, kp.secret(), 0, 40_000).expect("decrypt"),
        31337,
        "the surviving secret does not match the surviving public key"
    );
}

/// ⚠ **A second rejected review claim.** The same leg reported that
/// `onboarding::PERMANENT_DECRYPTION_CREDENTIAL` overstates the harm when it
/// says the master seed becomes a decryption credential *"for every confidential
/// amount this account ever holds, sends or receives"* — reading it as a claim
/// that `sk_A` decrypts **counterparties'** ciphertexts.
///
/// It is not that claim, and the sentence as written is true. Every confidential
/// amount this account is party to carries a ciphertext encrypted **to this
/// account's own key**: a Send it makes carries `SenderEncryptedAmount` under the
/// sender's key, and a Send it receives carries `DestinationEncryptedAmount`
/// under the destination's. So all three verbs hold, for this account's amounts,
/// under this account's `sk_A`.
///
/// The narrower thing the leg was reaching for is also true — `sk_A` does **not**
/// decrypt a counterparty's own ciphertext — and both halves are asserted here,
/// because a disclosure this project has to defend is worth one test.
#[test]
fn the_derived_key_decrypts_this_accounts_amounts_and_only_this_accounts() {
    let mine = keygen::derive_from_master_seed(V1_SEED, None).unwrap().into_keypair();
    let theirs = keygen::derive_from_master_seed(V2_SEED, None).unwrap().into_keypair();
    assert_ne!(mine.public(), theirs.public(), "the two vectors must differ");

    // One Send's ciphertext set, as `ConfidentialMPTSend` carries it: the same
    // `r` for every participant, one ciphertext per key.
    let r = blinding(13);
    let amount = 4321u64;
    let to_me = prover::encrypt(amount, mine.public(), &r).unwrap();
    let to_them = prover::encrypt(amount, theirs.public(), &r).unwrap();

    // "holds, sends or receives" — in each case there is a ciphertext under this
    // account's key, and the derived secret reads it.
    assert_eq!(prover::decrypt(&to_me, mine.secret(), 0, 9_000).unwrap(), amount);

    // And the boundary: the counterparty's own ciphertext is not ours to read,
    // even though it encrypts the same number under the same `r`.
    assert!(
        prover::decrypt(&to_them, mine.secret(), 0, 9_000).is_err(),
        "this account's secret decrypted a counterparty's ciphertext"
    );
    // The control, so the refusal above is not simply a broken decryptor.
    assert_eq!(prover::decrypt(&to_them, theirs.secret(), 0, 9_000).unwrap(), amount);
}

/// B-23: the two seed dispatches must agree.
///
/// ⚠ **This test exists because a duplicate was REMOVED.** `keygen::derive_raw`
/// used to reach the account key through `xrpl::AccountKey::from_family_seed`,
/// which decodes the seed a second time (`REVIEW-029a` finding 3). B-23 decodes
/// once and dispatches on the algorithm itself — which duplicates the two-arm
/// `match` that lives inside `from_family_seed`.
///
/// **The cost of removing a call is a copy of its body, and the risk is drift.**
/// This is the falsifier for that drift: both seed types, through both paths,
/// must produce the same AccountID. It fails if either dispatch gains an arm the
/// other lacks, or if the arms are ever swapped.
///
/// ⚠ **It is a real falsifier and that was checked by breaking it**, not
/// asserted — swapping the two arms in `derive_raw` makes it fail. `CLAIMS.md`
/// B-23.
#[test]
fn the_two_seed_dispatches_agree() {
    // secp256k1 and ed25519 — the only two the decoder produces.
    for seed in ["sp6JdwovBCsiwnMhXuvZGZtPUoGVj", "sEdSJHdnVumf99WfaHTnU8DaQkx5Q4n"] {
        let via_from_family_seed = wconf_signer::xrpl::AccountKey::from_family_seed(seed)
            .expect("a published seed must decode");

        // `derive_from_master_seed` carries `derive_raw`'s dispatch, and it
        // returns the AccountID that dispatch produced.
        let derived = wconf_signer::keygen::derive_from_master_seed(seed, None)
            .expect("the derivation is total once the input checks pass");

        assert_eq!(
            &via_from_family_seed.account_id(),
            derived.account_id(),
            "the two seed dispatches disagree for {seed}: keygen's own `match` on \
             `algo` has drifted from `AccountKey::from_family_seed`'s"
        );
    }
}
//! Known-answer tests for XRPL serialisation, addressing and signing.
//!
//! Architecture §6 step 3. Every expected value in
//! `tools/spike-step3-2026-08-18/xrpl-vectors.json` is produced by
//! `ripple-binary-codec@2.9.0`, `ripple-keypairs@2.0.0` and
//! `ripple-address-codec@5.0.1` — implementations this project did not write —
//! and the generator is committed beside them under **P-8**.
//!
//! This is the same discipline `tests/r_and_rho_kat.rs` applies to the ruled
//! derivation, for the same reason: **a value written from recollection rather
//! than computed is a defect this project has already shipped once**, and it
//! was the test that was wrong while the implementation was right.
//!
//! If the vectors file is missing this test **fails**. It does not skip. A test
//! that quietly passes when its evidence is absent is a false-pass mode, which
//! is precisely what `REVIEW-009` §8 found in `t-nbound.c`.

use serde_json::Value;
use wconf_signer::xrpl::{self, *};

fn vectors() -> Value {
    let path = std::path::Path::new(env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR"))
        .join("../tools/spike-step3-2026-08-18/xrpl-vectors.json");
    let raw = std::fs::read_to_string(&path).unwrap_or_else(|e| {
        panic!(
            "cannot read {}: {e}\n\
             Regenerate with: bun tools/spike-step3-2026-08-18/xrpl-vectors.ts --write\n\
             This test does not skip when its evidence is missing.",
            path.display()
        )
    });
    serde_json::from_str(&raw).expect("the vectors file is not valid JSON")
}

fn s(v: &Value, k: &str) -> String {
    v[k].as_str().unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("vector has no string `{k}`")).to_string()
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Addressing and key derivation
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

#[test]
fn family_seed_derivation_matches_ripple_keypairs() {
    let v = vectors();
    let keys = v["keys"].as_array().unwrap();
    assert!(!keys.is_empty(), "the vectors carry no keys");
    for k in keys {
        let key = AccountKey::from_family_seed(&s(k, "seed")).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(hex(key.public()), s(k, "public_key"), "public key for {}", s(k, "seed"));
        assert_eq!(hex(&key.account_id()), s(k, "account_id"));
        assert_eq!(key.address(), s(k, "address"));

        // The same seed reached through raw entropy.
        let entropy = unhex(&s(k, "entropy")).unwrap();
        let by_entropy = match s(k, "algorithm").as_str() {
            "ed25519" => AccountKey::ed25519_from_entropy(&entropy).unwrap(),
            _ => AccountKey::from_entropy(&entropy).unwrap(),
        };
        assert_eq!(hex(by_entropy.public()), s(k, "public_key"));
    }
    assert!(
        keys.iter().any(|k| k["algorithm"] == "ed25519"),
        "the vectors cover no ed25519 key, and every account this project holds on Devnet is one"
    );
}

/// The two algorithms sign **different things**, and the vectors are what says
/// which.
///
/// secp256k1 signs `SHA512Half(message)`; ed25519 signs `message`. Reasoning
/// about that is how it gets written backwards, so both are pinned against
/// `ripple-keypairs` signing the same probe.
///
/// ⚠ **On the DETERMINISTIC ARM — `CLAIMS.md` B-14 (2026-08-20) and B-16
/// (2026-08-27).** The default signing path is hedged on **both** algorithms
/// now, so neither can be pinned against `ripple-keypairs`, which is
/// deterministic:
///
/// * **secp256k1** — RFC 6979 with 32 bytes of fresh entropy as additional data.
/// * **ed25519** — the RFC 8032 nonce prefix XORed with `SHA512(DST ‖ aux)`.
///
/// ⚠ This paragraph described the secp256k1 half only, and went on to discuss
/// low-`S` and DER, while the loop below signs **every** vector key including
/// the ed25519 one. `REVIEW-026`, Codex leg.
///
/// **What this still pins is what it was ever really for:** the digest choice,
/// the serialisation, the low-`S` normalisation and the DER encoding, against
/// an implementation this project did not write. What it no longer covers —
/// that the *default* path produces valid signatures — is covered by
/**
 * xrpl-vectors.ts — the generator for `signer/tests/xrpl_vectors.rs`.
 *
 * Architecture §6 step 3 puts XRPL serialisation and signing inside the Rust
 * signer. Nothing in this repository had ever produced XRPL bytes from Rust, so
 * the encoder needed something to be wrong against. These vectors are it.
 *
 * **Committed under standing prohibition P-8:** a value whose generator is
 * unavailable is not checkable. This file is the generator; `xrpl-vectors.json`
 * is its output; the Rust test fails if the two disagree.
 *
 * Three independent implementations this project did not write produce every
 * expected value here:
 *
 *   - `ripple-binary-codec@2.9.0`   — canonical serialisation. CLAIMS.md E-46
 *                                      records it verified against a live
 *                                      node's `server_definitions` across all
 *                                      381 fields.
 *   - `ripple-keypairs@2.0.0`       — family-seed derivation and ECDSA.
 *   - `ripple-address-codec@5.0.1`  — base58check.
 *
 * Run it two ways:
 *
 *   bun tools/spike-step3-2026-08-18/xrpl-vectors.ts          # check, exit 1 on drift
 *   bun tools/spike-step3-2026-08-18/xrpl-vectors.ts --write  # regenerate
 *
 * ⚠ **`--write` is how a wrong encoder gets blessed.** Regenerate only when the
 * codec version changes, and say so in the commit. The whole value of a vector
 * is that it was not written by the thing it tests.
 *
 * No network. No keys that matter. Devnet only, and this file does not submit.
 */
import { encode, encodeForSigning } from "ripple-binary-codec";
import { deriveKeypair, deriveAddress, sign } from "ripple-keypairs";
import { decodeAccountID, encodeSeed } from "ripple-address-codec";
import { existsSync, readFileSync, writeFileSync } from "node:fs";

const OUT = new URL("./xrpl-vectors.json", import.meta.url).pathname;

const hex = (b: Uint8Array | Buffer) => Buffer.from(b).toString("hex").toUpperCase();
/// An arbitrary fixed message, signed by every key below. Nothing about it is
/// a transaction; it exists to pin what each algorithm hashes.
const PROBE = "5354580012005821000000022200000000";
const rep = (byte: number, n: number) => hex(Buffer.alloc(n, byte));

// --- 1. seeds -------------------------------------------------------------
// Deterministic entropy so the vectors are stable. These are NOT keys anybody
// should fund: the entropy is a constant published in this file.
// Both algorithms. Devnet's faucet hands out ed25519, so a signer that only
// spoke secp256k1 could not sign for any account this project already has.
const entropies = [0x00, 0x11, 0x7f, 0xff];
const keys = [
  ...entropies.map((b) => ({ b, algo: "secp256k1" as const })),
  ...entropies.map((b) => ({ b, algo: "ed25519" as const })),
].map(({ b, algo }) => {
  const entropy = Buffer.alloc(16, b);
  const seed = encodeSeed(entropy, algo);
  const kp = deriveKeypair(seed);
  const address = deriveAddress(kp.publicKey);
  return {
    seed,
    algorithm: algo,
    entropy: hex(entropy),
    public_key: kp.publicKey.toUpperCase(),
    account_id: hex(decodeAccountID(address)),
    address,
    // The same message signed by both, so the Rust side can pin that
    // secp256k1 signs SHA512Half(message) and ed25519 signs the message.
    signature_over_probe: sign(PROBE, kp.privateKey),
  };
});

// --- 2. transactions ------------------------------------------------------
// Field values are structurally correct — right lengths, right shapes — but
// they are NOT real proofs. These vectors pin the ENCODER, not the prover;
// `signer/tests/prover_pristine.rs` pins the prover.
const holder = keys[0];
const dest = keys[1];
const ed_holder = keys.find((k) => k.algorithm === "ed25519")!;

const txs: Record<string, any> = {
  send: {
    TransactionType: "ConfidentialMPTSend",
    Account: holder.address,
    Destination: dest.address,
    MPTokenIssuanceID: rep(0x5a, 24),
    SenderEncryptedAmount: rep(0x01, 66),
    DestinationEncryptedAmount: rep(0x02, 66),
    IssuerEncryptedAmount: rep(0x03, 66),
    AmountCommitment: rep(0x04, 33),
    BalanceCommitment: rep(0x05, 33),
    ZKProof: rep(0x06, 946),
    Sequence: 12345,
    Fee: "5000",
    Flags: 0,
    LastLedgerSequence: 9876543,
    NetworkID: 2,
    SigningPubKey: holder.public_key,
  },
  convert: {
//! XRPL binary serialisation, address handling and transaction signing.
//!
//! Architecture §6 step 3. This replaces the length-framed placeholder that
//! `txn.rs` used while nothing signed, and it is the first code in this
//! repository that produces bytes intended for a network. **Devnet only.**
//!
//! # How this is checked, because "it looked right" is not a check
//!
//! Every claim this module makes is pinned by
//! `tests/xrpl_vectors.rs` against vectors generated by
//! `ripple-binary-codec@2.9.0`, `ripple-keypairs@2.0.0` and
//! `ripple-address-codec@5.0.1` — three independently written implementations
//! this project did not author. The generator is committed at
//! `tools/spike-step3-2026-08-18/xrpl-vectors.ts` under standing prohibition
//! **P-8**: a value whose generator is unavailable is not checkable. The codec
//! itself is verified against a live node's `server_definitions` across all 381
//! fields (`CLAIMS.md` E-46).
//!
//! An expected value written from recollection rather than computed is the
//! exact defect this project shipped once already. Nothing here is recalled.
//!
//! # ⚠ The ECDSA signing nonce is a fifth randomness site, and it is RULED
//!
//! ~~and no packet has covered it. This module uses RFC 6979 deterministic
//! `k` … that is a defensible default and it is not a ruling.~~ **Struck
//! 2026-08-20 — `REVIEW-018` ruled `k`, and this header outlived it by a day.**
//! It described the module as using pure RFC 6979 while the code below had
//! already been changed to hedge, which is the ninth instance of the failure
//! `lib.rs` keeps a running record of: *a statement that is true when written
//! is false by the end of the same session.*
//!
//! What holds now: sigma nonces (REVIEW-005c), `r` and `ρ` (REVIEW-010c),
//! ElGamal keygen (REVIEW-012c) and the transaction's ECDSA `k` (REVIEW-018)
//! all have rulings; the Bulletproof's internal scalars are issue #8 and are
//! **open**. This module signs secp256k1 with **hedged RFC 6979** — the
//! deterministic construction plus 32 bytes of fresh entropy as additional
//! data — because the issue #1 ruling extends here and neither leg found a
//! principled distinction from the sigma nonces.
//!
//! ⚠ **Entropy failure DEGRADES to pure RFC 6979; it does not refuse.** That is
//! the sigma-nonce rule, and importing `r`/`ρ`'s "refuse to sign" here was
//! recommended by a leg and rejected. ~~⚠ **Ruled for secp256k1 only** —
//! ed25519 is deterministic by construction, it is the live path for every
//! account this project holds, and it is `CLAIMS.md` **B-16**, open.~~
//!
//! # ⚠ Ed25519 is hedged too — B-16, ruled 2026-08-27, BUILT and EXECUTED
//!
//! ~~**B-16 IS RULED … AND THIS FILE DOES NOT IMPLEMENT IT.**~~ **Struck
//! 2026-08-27, in the session that built it.** Three independent legs
//! (`REVIEW-024`) ruled that the issue #1 extension holds for the ed25519 nonce
//! and that the residual is **not** accepted. Both algorithms hedge now.
//!
//! ⚠ **BUILT AND EXECUTED IS NOT REVIEWED, and they are different things** — the
//! distinction `REVIEW-024` §6 drew between *ruled* and *built*, one step along.
//! All eight of §6's execution gates have run, including the Devnet transaction,
//! so *"the signer hedges ed25519"* is the accurate sentence. §7 item 4 —
//! **two adversarial rounds, the second reviewing the fixes** — is what is still
//! in flight; `REVIEW-025` is round one and both external legs said not to
//! write "closed" before round two. They were right and this paragraph is the
//! correction.
//!
//! The construction is normative in
//! `docs/reviews/REVIEW-024-RULING-ed25519-nonce-2026-08-27.md` §3 — **where
//! this file and the ruling disagree, the ruling wins**:
//!
//! ```text
//!   h   := SHA512(sk)          p := h[32..64]          a := clamp(h[0..32])
//!   DST := b"WCONF/ed25519-nonce-hedge/v1\0"          (29 bytes, fixed)
//!   q   := p XOR SHA512(DST ‖ aux)[0..32]     when the mask is applied
//!   q   := p                                  when it is not
//! ```
//!
//! and then **exactly RFC 8032 with `q` in place of `p`**, evaluated by
//! `ed25519_dalek::hazmat::raw_sign::<Sha512>`. The scalar, its clamping, the
//! public key, the base point, the challenge transcript `R ‖ A ‖ M` and the
//! encodings are untouched; **this project writes no elliptic-curve
//! arithmetic**. Nothing auxiliary goes on the wire — the signature is 64
//! ordinary bytes and any conforming verifier accepts it.
//!
//! ⚠ **A successful draw of thirty-two ZERO bytes stays hedged.** Only a
//! *reported* entropy failure omits the mask, and then the output is
//! byte-identical RFC 8032. `REVIEW-018` §4a got the mirror of this wrong and it
//! cost a round.
//!
//! ⚠ **`a_successful_all_zero_draw_still_hedges` is what holds that**, and this
//! comment credited `zero_aux_is_hedged_not_a_fallback` for one commit —
//! **which is the test that cannot see it**, because it enters at
//! `sign_with_aux` and the decision is made one function earlier in
//! [`fresh_aux`]. Reverting `fresh_aux` left all 263 tests green. Found by the
//! §6 item 8 revert sweep, not by reading; the miscredit was found by

   165	    } catch (e) {
   166	      return status(e instanceof LedgerRefusal ? 502 : 503, { error: String(e) });
   167	    }
   168	  })
   169	
   170	  /**
   171	   * What the ISSUER sees. ⚠ P-9: no configuration makes a holder private from
   172	   * the issuer, and showing this is the honest counterpart to showing the
   173	   * observer view.
   174	   */
   175	  .get("/issuer/:account/:issuanceId", ({ params }) =>
   176	    // ⚠ **No ledger read, no key, no decryption.** This reads the CLI tool's
   177	    // output file. The route that decrypted per request was an oracle (E-212).
   178	    issuerView(params.account, params.issuanceId))
   179	
   180	  /**
     1	/**
     2	 * What the ISSUER sees — read from the CLI tool's OUTPUT, never decrypted here.
     3	 *
     4	 * ⚠ **`CLAIMS.md` P-9: a holder is never private from the issuer.** The
     5	 * decryption mirror is mandatory and no configuration makes this false. Showing
     6	 * the issuer's view beside the observer's is the honest counterpart to showing
     7	 * what the public sees.
     8	 *
     9	 * # ⚠⚠ THIS MODULE HELD THE ISSUER KEY AND DECRYPTED PER REQUEST. THAT WAS A
    10	 * DECRYPTION ORACLE, AND IT VIOLATED THIS MILESTONE'S OWN SCOPE.
    11	 *
    12	 * Architecture §6 step 8 — written by the session that then broke it — says the
    13	 * tier **may touch** *"the issuer-view decryption tool's **output**, given a
    14	 * demo issuer key handled as the secret it is"* and **may never touch** *"any
    15	 * private key"*. `CLAUDE.md` says the issuer-view tool is *"a single CLI
    16	 * command … **Not a standalone application**."*
    17	 *
    18	 * The first version read `WCONF_ELGAMAL_SK` from the environment and decrypted
    19	 * on demand. ⚠ **An unauthenticated `GET` where the caller chooses the account
    20	 * and receives a decrypted balance is an oracle** — CORS constrains browsers,
    21	 * not `curl`, and `/meta`'s `holdsKeys: false` was false while it ran. It is
    22	 * the **fourth rotation trigger `ADR-0003` names**, written by the same session
    23	 * one file over (`CLAIMS.md` E-212).
    24	 *
    25	 * ⚠ **And the helper it used passed the secret through `argv`** —
    26	 * `decryptInRange` spawns `mptool decrypt <ct> <sk> …`, and argv is
    27	 * world-readable via `ps`: the precise exposure the issuer CLI refuses a
    28	 * `--key` flag to avoid.
    29	 *
    30	 * # What it is now
    31	 *
    32	 * **A file reader.** `tools/issuer-view-snapshot.ts` holds the key, decrypts the
    33	 * curated demo set in-process via `@xrplf/mpt-crypto`, and writes results. This
    34	 * module reads those results. ⚠ **There is no key here, no decryption, no
    35	 * subprocess and no attacker-chosen input** — the oracle is *removed*, not
    36	 * guarded, so there is nothing to authenticate.
    37	 */
    38	
    39	import { readFileSync } from "node:fs";
    40	
    41	/**
    42	 * ⚠ **Named here so `check-web-readonly` can assert the web tier never READS
    43	 * it.** The variable belongs to the CLI tool; this constant exists to be
    44	 * checked against, not to be consulted.
    45	 */
    46	export const FORBIDDEN_SECRET_ENV = "WCONF_ELGAMAL_SK";
    47	
    48	export const ISSUER_KEY_POLICY =
    49	  "This process holds NO issuer key, performs NO decryption, and DOES NOT SERVE a decrypted " +
    50	  "balance. The key lives only in tools/issuer-view-snapshot.ts, which reads it from the " +
    51	  "environment — never a flag, because argv is world-readable via `ps` — decrypts a curated set " +
    52	  "in-process, and writes results to a file it creates with mode 0600. ⚠ This tier reads that file " +
    53	  "and forwards WHETHER a balance was decryptable, never the figure. " +
    54	  "⚠⚠ Serving the figure was a disclosure boundary, not a signing one: loopback is not uid-scoped, " +
    55	  "so an unauthenticated GET handed every local user a value the producing tool had just " +
    56	  "restricted to one (CLAIMS.md E-228, ruled and removed E-241). There is no request that reaches " +
    57	  "a decryption and none that returns one. The figure is in tools/issuer-decrypt-balance.ts, a CLI " +
    58	  "command, which is where CLAUDE.md says the issuer view belongs.";
    59	
    60	/** The ledger field the snapshot tool decrypts. Named once, asserted in tests. */
    61	export const MIRROR_FIELD_LEDGER_NAME = "IssuerEncryptedBalance" as const;
    62	
    63	/**
    64	 * ⚠⚠ **READ LAZILY, AND THAT IS A CORRECTNESS FIX RATHER THAN A STYLE ONE.**
    65	 *
    66	 * As a module-level `const` this bound at import time — so a test that set
    67	 * `WCONF_ISSUER_SNAPSHOT` afterwards was ignored, and a `await import()` handed
    68	 * back the **cached** module rather than re-reading it. The refusal test
    69	 * therefore pointed at the DEFAULT path, which is absent in a clean checkout,
    70	 * so `issuerView` returned unavailable for that reason and the test passed
    71	 * **whether or not the refusal existed**. ⚠ **Measured: deleting both refusal
    72	 * lines left all 46 tests green** (`CLAIMS.md` E-215).
    73	 *
    74	 * ⚠ **Fifth instance in this repository of a check built to catch false passes
    75	 * false-passing** (E-165, E-168, E-182, E-203, E-207) — and this one was
    76	 * written in the round that fixed a false pass.
    77	 */
    78	function snapshotPath(): string {
    79	  return process.env.WCONF_ISSUER_SNAPSHOT ?? "/tmp/wconf/issuer-view-snapshot.json";
    80	}
    81	
    82	export interface IssuerViewResult {
    83	  available: boolean;
    84	  reason?: string;
    85	  keyPolicy: string;
    86	  account: string;
    87	  issuanceId: string;
    88	  mirrorField?: string;
    89	  /**
    90	   * ⚠⚠ **`decrypted` IS NO LONGER SERVED — `CLAIMS.md` E-241, ruled 2026-09-05.**
    91	   *
    92	   * The snapshot still holds it; **this API does not forward it.** The tool that
    93	   * produces the snapshot writes it `mode 0o600`, and serving its contents over
    94	   * an unauthenticated loopback listener handed every local uid a value the
    95	   * project had just restricted to one — `REVIEW-036` U1, `CLAIMS.md` E-228.
    96	   *
    97	   * ⚠ **The remedy is E-212's own: REMOVE the capability rather than guard it.**
    98	   * There is no request that reaches a decryption **and none that returns one**,
    99	   * so there is still nothing to authenticate.
   100	   *
   101	   * ⚠ **What the view keeps is the point it was making.** That the issuer CAN
   102	   * read this balance is proved by `mirrorField` being present and populated on
   103	   * the ledger — P-9, which no configuration removes. **The plaintext integer
   104	   * demonstrated only that THIS PROJECT holds the demo issuer key**, which
   105	   * nobody doubted. The integer lives where `CLAUDE.md` says it belongs: in
   106	   * `tools/issuer-decrypt-balance.ts`, a CLI command, not a served field.
   107	   */
   108	  decryptedWithheld: true;
   109	  /** ⚠ Whether the tool COULD decrypt it. The fact, not the figure. */
   110	  decryptable?: boolean;
   111	  searchedTo?: string;
   112	  publicAmount?: string;
   113	  /** ⚠ When the snapshot was taken. Balances move; a stale number is a lie by omission. */
   114	  snapshotAtUnix?: number;
   115	  snapshotAgeNote?: string;
   116	  whatThisProves: string;
   117	}
   118	
   119	interface Snapshot {
   120	  producedAtUnix?: number;
   121	  entries?: Record<string, any>;
   122	}
   123	
   124	function loadSnapshot(): Snapshot | null {
   125	  try {
   126	    const parsed = JSON.parse(readFileSync(snapshotPath(), "utf8"));
   127	    // ⚠ **A snapshot carrying anything key-shaped is REFUSED, not filtered.**
   128	    // The tool writes results only; a file that does not look like that is not
   129	    // a file this tier should be reading.
   130	    const raw = JSON.stringify(parsed);
   131	    // ⚠ Built from the constant, not inlined. A regex literal is not a string
   132	    // literal, so an inlined spelling survives the gate's comment/literal
   133	    // stripping and reads as a genuine secret reference — which is exactly what
   134	    // it did on the first run of part 4.
   135	    if (/"(sk|secret|privateKey|elgamal_sk|seed)"\s*:/i.test(raw)) return null;
   136	    if (new RegExp(`\\b${FORBIDDEN_SECRET_ENV}\\b`).test(raw)) return null;
   137	    return parsed;
   138	  } catch {
   139	    return null;
   140	  }
   141	}
   142	
   143	export function issuerView(account: string, issuanceId: string): IssuerViewResult {
   144	  const base = {
   145	    decryptedWithheld: true as const,
   146	    keyPolicy: ISSUER_KEY_POLICY,
   147	    account,
   148	    issuanceId,
   149	    whatThisProves:
   150	      "⚠ That the issuer can read this holder's balance, always. P-9 is not a limitation of this " +
   151	      "demo — the decryption mirror is part of the protocol and no configuration removes it. A " +
   152	      "holder is confidential from the PUBLIC, never from the ISSUER.",
   153	  };
   154	
   155	  const snap = loadSnapshot();
   156	  if (!snap?.entries) {
   157	    return {
   158	      ...base,
   159	      available: false,
   160	      reason:
   161	        `No issuer-view snapshot at ${snapshotPath()}. ⚠ This is a NAMED GAP in the view, not ` +
   162	        `evidence that the issuer cannot decrypt — P-9 says it can. Produce one with: ` +
   163	        `WCONF_ELGAMAL_SK=<64 hex> bun run tools/issuer-view-snapshot.ts`,
   164	    };
   165	  }
   166	
   167	  const entry = snap.entries[`${account}/${issuanceId}`];
   168	  if (!entry) {
   169	    return {
   170	      ...base,
   171	      available: false,
   172	      snapshotAtUnix: snap.producedAtUnix,
   173	      reason:
   174	        `This account is not in the snapshot. ⚠ The snapshot covers a CURATED demo set, not every ` +
   175	        `account — that bound is what removes the attacker-chosen input, and it is deliberate. ` +
   176	        `Add it to web/api/demo-accounts.ts and re-run the tool.`,
   177	    };
   178	  }
   179	
   180	  return {
   181	    ...base,
   182	    available: Boolean(entry.available),
   183	    reason: entry.reason,
   184	    mirrorField: entry.mirrorField,
   185	    // ⚠⚠ E-241: `entry.decrypted` is READ and deliberately NOT forwarded. Only
   186	    // whether it exists crosses the boundary, never its value.
   187	    decryptedWithheld: true,
   188	    decryptable: entry.decrypted !== undefined,
   189	    searchedTo: entry.searchedTo,
   190	    publicAmount: entry.publicAmount,
   191	    snapshotAtUnix: snap.producedAtUnix,
   192	    snapshotAgeNote:
   193	      "⚠ A SNAPSHOT, not a live read. Balances move; this is what the issuer saw when the tool ran.",
   194	  };
   195	}

Sync destination repository from source repository. Syncing uses the default branch
of the source repository to update the matching branch on the destination
repository so they are equal. A fast forward update will be used except when the
`--force` flag is specified, then the two branches will
be synced using a hard reset.

Without an argument, the local repository is selected as the destination repository.

The source repository is the parent of the destination repository by default.
This can be overridden with the `--source` flag.


USAGE
  gh repo sync [<destination-repository>] [flags]

FLAGS
  -b, --branch string   Branch to sync (default [default branch])
      --force           Hard reset the branch of the destination repository to match the source repository
  -s, --source string   Source repository

INHERITED FLAGS
  --help   Show help for command

EXAMPLES
  # Sync local repository from remote parent
  $ gh repo sync
  
  # Sync local repository from remote parent on specific branch
  $ gh repo sync --branch v1
  
  # Sync remote fork from its parent
  $ gh repo sync owner/cli-fork
  
  # Sync remote repository from another remote repository
  $ gh repo sync owner/repo --source owner2/repo2

//! Known-answer tests for XRPL serialisation, addressing and signing.
//!
//! Architecture §6 step 3. Every expected value in
//! `tools/spike-step3-2026-08-18/xrpl-vectors.json` is produced by
//! `ripple-binary-codec@2.9.0`, `ripple-keypairs@2.0.0` and
//! `ripple-address-codec@5.0.1` — implementations this project did not write —
//! and the generator is committed beside them under **P-8**.
//!
//! This is the same discipline `tests/r_and_rho_kat.rs` applies to the ruled
//! derivation, for the same reason: **a value written from recollection rather
//! than computed is a defect this project has already shipped once**, and it
//! was the test that was wrong while the implementation was right.
//!
//! If the vectors file is missing this test **fails**. It does not skip. A test
//! that quietly passes when its evidence is absent is a false-pass mode, which
//! is precisely what `REVIEW-009` §8 found in `t-nbound.c`.

use serde_json::Value;
use wconf_signer::xrpl::{self, *};

        .join("../tools/spike-step3-2026-08-18/xrpl-vectors.json");
    let raw = std::fs::read_to_string(&path).unwrap_or_else(|e| {
        panic!(
            "cannot read {}: {e}\n\
/**
 * xrpl-vectors.ts — the generator for `signer/tests/xrpl_vectors.rs`.
 *
 * Architecture §6 step 3 puts XRPL serialisation and signing inside the Rust
 * signer. Nothing in this repository had ever produced XRPL bytes from Rust, so
 * the encoder needed something to be wrong against. These vectors are it.
 *
 * **Committed under standing prohibition P-8:** a value whose generator is
 * unavailable is not checkable. This file is the generator; `xrpl-vectors.json`
 * is its output; the Rust test fails if the two disagree.
 *
 * Three independent implementations this project did not write produce every
 * expected value here:
 *
 *   - `ripple-binary-codec@2.9.0`   — canonical serialisation. CLAIMS.md E-46
 *                                      records it verified against a live
 *                                      node's `server_definitions` across all
 *                                      381 fields.
 *   - `ripple-keypairs@2.0.0`       — family-seed derivation and ECDSA.
 *   - `ripple-address-codec@5.0.1`  — base58check.
 *
 * Run it two ways:
 *
 *   bun tools/spike-step3-2026-08-18/xrpl-vectors.ts          # check, exit 1 on drift
 *   bun tools/spike-step3-2026-08-18/xrpl-vectors.ts --write  # regenerate
 *
 * ⚠ **`--write` is how a wrong encoder gets blessed.** Regenerate only when the
 * codec version changes, and say so in the commit. The whole value of a vector
 * is that it was not written by the thing it tests.

 * No network. No keys that matter. Devnet only, and this file does not submit.

//!
//! An expected value written from recollection rather than computed is the
//! exact defect this project shipped once already. Nothing here is recalled.
//!
//! # ⚠ The ECDSA signing nonce is a fifth randomness site, and it is RULED
//!
//! ~~and no packet has covered it. This module uses RFC 6979 deterministic
//! `k` … that is a defensible default and it is not a ruling.~~ **Struck
//! 2026-08-20 — `REVIEW-018` ruled `k`, and this header outlived it by a day.**
//! It described the module as using pure RFC 6979 while the code below had
//! already been changed to hedge, which is the ninth instance of the failure
//! `lib.rs` keeps a running record of: *a statement that is true when written
//! is false by the end of the same session.*
//!
//! What holds now: sigma nonces (REVIEW-005c), `r` and `ρ` (REVIEW-010c),
//! ElGamal keygen (REVIEW-012c) and the transaction's ECDSA `k` (REVIEW-018)
//! all have rulings; the Bulletproof's internal scalars are issue #8 and are
//! **open**. This module signs secp256k1 with **hedged RFC 6979** — the
//! deterministic construction plus 32 bytes of fresh entropy as additional
//! data — because the issue #1 ruling extends here and neither leg found a
//! principled distinction from the sigma nonces.
//!
//! ⚠ **Entropy failure DEGRADES to pure RFC 6979; it does not refuse.** That is
//! the sigma-nonce rule, and importing `r`/`ρ`'s "refuse to sign" here was
//! recommended by a leg and rejected. ~~⚠ **Ruled for secp256k1 only** —
//! ed25519 is deterministic by construction, it is the live path for every
//! account this project holds, and it is `CLAIMS.md` **B-16**, open.~~
//!

	if err != nil {
		return err
	}
	apiClient := api.NewClientFromHTTP(httpClient)

	var destRepo, srcRepo ghrepo.Interface

	destRepo, err = ghrepo.FromFullName(opts.DestArg)
	if err != nil {
		return err
	}

	if opts.SrcArg != "" {
		srcRepo, err = ghrepo.FromFullName(opts.SrcArg)
		if err != nil {
			return err
		}
	}

	if srcRepo != nil && destRepo.RepoHost() != srcRepo.RepoHost() {
		return fmt.Errorf("can't sync repositories from different hosts")
	}

	opts.IO.StartProgressIndicator()
	baseBranchLabel, err := executeRemoteRepoSync(apiClient, destRepo, srcRepo, opts)
	opts.IO.StopProgressIndicator()
	if err != nil {
		if errors.Is(err, divergingError) {
			return fmt.Errorf("can't sync because there are diverging changes; use `--force` to overwrite the destination branch")
		}
		return err
	}

	if opts.IO.IsStdoutTTY() {
		cs := opts.IO.ColorScheme()
		branchName := opts.Branch
		if _, after, ok := strings.Cut(baseBranchLabel, ":"); ok {
			branchName = after
		}
		fmt.Fprintf(opts.IO.Out, "%s Synced the \"%s:%s\" branch from \"%s\"\n",
			cs.SuccessIcon(),
			destRepo.RepoOwner(),
			branchName,
			baseBranchLabel)
	}

	return nil
}

var divergingError = errors.New("diverging changes")

func executeLocalRepoSync(srcRepo ghrepo.Interface, remote string, opts *SyncOptions) error {
	git := opts.Git
	branch := opts.Branch
	useForce := opts.Force

	hasLocalBranch := git.HasLocalBranch(branch)
	if hasLocalBranch {
		fastForward, err := git.IsAncestor(branch, "FETCH_HEAD")
		if err != nil {
			return err
		}
		if !fastForward && !useForce {
			return divergingError
		}
		if fastForward && useForce {
			useForce = false
		}
	}

	currentBranch, err := git.CurrentBranch()
	if err != nil && !errors.Is(err, gitpkg.ErrNotOnAnyBranch) {
		return err
	}
	if currentBranch == branch {
		if isDirty, err := git.IsDirty(); err == nil && isDirty {
			return fmt.Errorf("refusing to sync due to uncommitted/untracked local changes\ntip: use `git stash --all` before retrying the sync and run `git stash pop` afterwards")
		} else if err != nil {
			return err
		}
		if useForce {
			if err := git.ResetHard("FETCH_HEAD"); err != nil {
				return err
			}
		} else {
			if err := git.MergeFastForward("FETCH_HEAD"); err != nil {
				return err
			}
		}
	} else {
		if hasLocalBranch {
			if err := git.UpdateBranch(branch, "FETCH_HEAD"); err != nil {
				worktrees, worktreeErr := git.Worktrees()
				if worktreeErr != nil {
					return fmt.Errorf("cannot update branch %q: %w; also cannot retrieve worktrees: %w", branch, err, worktreeErr)
				}
				if worktree := gitpkg.WorktreeForBranch(worktrees, branch); worktree != nil {
					if worktree.Prunable {
						return fmt.Errorf("can't sync %q because stale worktree metadata still associates it with %s\ntip: run `git worktree prune` and retry", branch, worktree.Path)
					}
					return fmt.Errorf("can't sync %q because it's checked out in another worktree at %s\ntip: run `gh repo sync` from that worktree instead", branch, worktree.Path)
				}
				return err
			}
		} else {
			if err := git.CreateBranch(branch, "FETCH_HEAD", fmt.Sprintf("%s/%s", remote, branch)); err != nil {
				return err
			}
		}
	}

	return nil
}

// ExecuteRemoteRepoSync will take several steps to sync the source and destination repositories.
// First it will try to use the merge-upstream API endpoint. If this fails due to merge conflicts
// or unknown merge issues then it will fallback to using the low level git references API endpoint.
// The reason the fallback is necessary is to better support these error cases. The git references API
// endpoint allows us to sync repositories that are not fast-forward merge compatible. Additionally,
// the git references API endpoint gives more detailed error responses as to why the sync failed.
// Unless the --force flag is specified we will not perform non-fast-forward merges.

// Unless the --force flag is specified we will not perform non-fast-forward merges.
func executeRemoteRepoSync(client *api.Client, destRepo, srcRepo ghrepo.Interface, opts *SyncOptions) (string, error) {
	branchName := opts.Branch
	if branchName == "" {
		var err error
		branchName, err = api.RepoDefaultBranch(client, destRepo)
		if err != nil {
			return "", err
		}
	}

	if baseBranch, err := triggerUpstreamMerge(client, destRepo, branchName); err == nil {
		return baseBranch, nil
	} else if _, ok := errors.AsType[upstreamMergeErr](err); !ok {
		return "", err
	}

	if srcRepo == nil {
		var err error
		srcRepo, err = api.RepoParent(client, destRepo)
		if err != nil {
			return "", err
		}
		if srcRepo == nil {
			return "", fmt.Errorf("can't determine source repository for %s because repository is not fork", ghrepo.FullName(destRepo))
		}
	}

	commit, err := latestCommit(client, srcRepo, branchName)
	if err != nil {
		return "", err
	}

	// Using string comparison is a brittle way to determine the error returned by the API
	// endpoint but unfortunately the API returns 422 for many reasons so we must
	// interpret the message provide better error messaging for our users.
	err = syncFork(client, destRepo, branchName, commit.Object.SHA, opts.Force)
	if err != nil {
		if httpErr, ok := errors.AsType[api.HTTPError](err); ok {
			switch httpErr.Message {
			case notFastForwardErrorMessage:
				return "", divergingError
			case branchDoesNotExistErrorMessage:
				return "", fmt.Errorf("%s branch does not exist on %s repository", branchName, ghrepo.FullName(destRepo))
			}
		}
		return "", err
	}

	return fmt.Sprintf("%s:%s", srcRepo.RepoOwner(), branchName), nil
}

    15	
    16	## Why the signer is a separate binary
    17	
    18	Because a browser page must not be able to reach a spending operation, and the cheapest way to guarantee that is to make it **impossible** rather than **forbidden**.
    19	
    20	`signer/src/ipc.rs` is a **Unix domain socket**. No web API opens one. So the XSS-to-spend-oracle path is closed by the transport, not by a header check or a token.
    21	
    22	⚠ **The alternative was designed and killed.** ADR-0001 rev 2 proposed localhost HTTP with an origin allowlist and a pasted session token. `REVIEW-003b` found the flaw and architecture §3 rule 3 records it as **withdrawn**: **an origin allowlist constrains pages, not processes.** Any local process can forge an `Origin` header, and the project's threat model puts a hostile same-host process **in scope**. A value-shaped interface plus a leaked token is a spend oracle.
    23	
    24	⚠ **What the socket does not buy, stated because it is the half people drop:** it does not stop a hostile process running as the same user. That process passes the kernel's peer-UID check because it genuinely is the holder's user. **The per-transaction confirmation is the boundary**, not the transport.
    25	
    26	---
    27	
    28	## Why the web tier is read-only
    29	
    30	⚠⚠ **THIS SECTION SAID *"Because it is the only version that needed no adversarial review"* AND THE REVIEW HAS SINCE RUN AND DISAGREED. RETRACTED 2026-09-02 — `CLAIMS.md` E-230, `REVIEW-036`.**
    31	
    32	~~*"A tier that reads public state and holds no credential grants no authority that did not already exist — so nothing moves, so the gate does not fire."*~~ **STRUCK.** All four legs of `REVIEW-036` ruled the claim **unsound as stated**:
    33	
    34	- ⚠ **"reads public state" is false.** `Request::Status` returns the randomness ledger's entry count, and `signer/src/store.rs:753-756` commits a record *"on success **and** on any local failure before emission — a cancelled confirmation, a failed proof, an aborted attempt."* **Public ledger history cannot reveal a declined confirmation.**
    35	- ⚠ **"holds no credential" is true and is not the property the argument needs.** It is not the same as *conveys nothing a credential produces*: the tier serves `decrypted` confidential balances out of a file written `mode 0600`, unauthenticated, over a loopback listener **that is not uid-scoped** (E-228).
    36	- ⚠ **The inference is invalid.** A trust boundary also governs confidentiality, transport exposure and **presentation integrity** — and E-209 is this project's own proof of the last, where the tier rendered a Docker-hosted Gitea's `303` as its own API response.
    37	
    38	**What is true, and it is what the milestone actually needed:** ⚠ **the tier adds no SIGNING authority.** It cannot construct `Pair` or `Prepare`, holds no key, and cannot reach the holder's confirmation — verified by both repo-access legs and by `check-web-readonly` parts 1-3. **`ADR-0003` proved that, and then wrote it as a universal about *authority*, which its evidence does not carry.**
    39	
    40	**The rotation triggers are now FIVE.** The four that were listed — a browser path to signing; keys or proving in the page; a native shell that opens the socket; **a tier that accepts an issuer key over HTTP** — plus the one the list was missing and which had **already fired**:
    41	
    42	> ⚠⚠ **EGRESS. If this tier makes any datum reachable by a party who could not obtain that datum before the tier existed, the boundary has MOVED — regardless of whether the tier signs, holds a key, or performs any computation.**
    43	
   229	   ⚠ **One thing step 7 turned up that ADR-0002 does not contain, and it is a disclosure error in our own document**: `CLAIMS.md` **F-24**. The facility walkthrough said a ConvertBack publishes *"the aggregate change"*. `MPTAmount` is a plaintext field on **every individual** ConvertBack, so retiring a facility in tranches publishes **every tranche size**. Corrected in the walkthrough; recorded because it survived that document's own `claims-check`.
   230	
   231	   *Falsified if:* the facility is built against a remote ledger endpoint while B-15's gate is shut, or if any part of it is described as hiding who transacts, when, or how often.
   232	
   233	8. **The web tier — a READ-ONLY, multi-role demonstration surface.** Bun · TanStack Start · Elysia · Tailwind · shadcn-idiom components. **SCOPED, BUILT AND EXERCISED 2026-08-28** — `web/api/` (Elysia, read-only) and `web/app/` (TanStack Start), **89 tests**, both type-checks clean, `make check-web-readonly` is the seventh gate, and the observer view was read against **live Devnet state** under the remote risk acceptance: two accounts at issuance `004625C9…FEE332CB25E8`, **`ConfidentialBalanceInbox` publicly identifiable as exactly zero on both** while `ConfidentialBalanceSpending` was non-zero on the same code path — **F-1 demonstrated live rather than asserted.** ⚠ **NOT under B-15's loopback condition**; the view is stamped `underB15Conditions: false` and says so on the page. **The scoping began with a correction**: ⚠ **`CLAUDE.md` told every new session that *"step 8 was written on 2026-08-26"* and it never was.** §6 had seven steps, §7 followed step 7 directly, and `grep -n "step 8"` over this document returned nothing. So the web tier had **no scope, no falsifier and no gate in the authority document** while the file every session reads first asserted that it did. **A scope note needs a re-check date exactly as much as an amendment claim does** — this document's own lesson about the `xrpl.js` blocker paragraph, landing one section over (`CLAIMS.md` **E-204**).
   234	
   235	   **What the step is, in one sentence: it renders what is already readable, and it adds no SIGNING authority.** ⚠⚠ **THIS READ *"and it adds no authority"* UNTIL 2026-09-02, AND THAT IS THE CLAIM `REVIEW-036` RETRACTED** (`CLAIMS.md` E-230): the tier serves a decrypted issuer view and signer-local status, unauthenticated, to **any local uid**, out from behind `0600`/`0700` mode bits the project sets deliberately (E-228). **A DISCLOSURE boundary moved. The signing claim is the sound one and is the one this step needs.** Four decisions were put to the project owner during scoping and ruled; they are recorded in **`ADR-0003`** and summarised here because §6 is where a step's shape lives.
   236	
   237	   - **D1 — the tier is read-only and never signs.** Browser → Elysia over localhost HTTP → a rippled node over JSON-RPC, plus **one** call into the signer's Unix socket: `Request::Status`, and nothing else. **Signing stays in the `wconf-signer` CLI**, where the holder confirms in the signer's own UI against parsed transaction fields (step 1's falsifier). ⚠⚠ ~~**This is the entire reason step 8 is buildable without an adversarial rotation** — it moves no trust boundary, because it introduces no new authority.~~ **STRUCK 2026-09-02 by `REVIEW-036`** — the rotation ran and ruled that reasoning unsound. **What is true: it introduces no new SIGNING authority**, and that is a narrower statement than *no boundary moves*. ⚠ **The rotation this sentence said was unnecessary is the one that falsified it.** The moment any path in this tier can reach a signing operation, it is a different design and §3's rotation requirement applies **before** implementation.
   238	   - **D2 — the browser wallet is prove-and-contrast, not a participant.** Connect a wallet, prove account control, show that account's **observer view**, and then show the capability matrix explaining what it cannot do. ⚠ **A browser wallet cannot perform a confidential operation**, and that is the demonstration rather than a gap: the operation needs the ElGamal secret **and** the XRPL account key; under the ruled keygen (`REVIEW-012`) the ElGamal secret is **derived from the master seed**; and a wallet extension exists precisely in order not to export seed material. **A fourth reason — that the extension's bundled codec does not know the XLS-0096 types — is asserted by no primary source this repository holds and is carried as a NAMED GAP, not as a premise.**
   239	   - **D3 — TanStack Start is kept, and the split is what makes it safe.** The capital-markets case is multi-role — issuer, auditor, supplier, facility operator, holder — and file-based routing with loaders is what lets those grow without a rewrite. SSR crosses no *new* boundary, because Elysia is already a local process reading holder data. ⚠ **The caveat makes the gate matter MORE, not less: server functions are exactly the one-line mechanism by which a later session adds a signing call.** Only the Elysia process may open the socket.
   240	   - **D4 — Ripple Custody is a research document and a simulated demo mode**, never an integration. This repository holds **no** Metaco Harmonize primary sources; every Harmonize specific is a dated named gap.
   241	
   242	   **What this step may touch:** public ledger state at a pinned validated index; the observer harness's view; the issuer-view decryption tool's output, given a demo issuer key handled as the secret it is; `Request::Status`; the facility's run records; and this repository's own documents.
   243	
   121	and `CONTRIBUTING.md` tells them what a good PR looks like.

    35	## 2. What has changed since REVIEW-002 — the reviewer needs all six
    36	
    37	REVIEW-002 was 2026-08-13. Six things have landed since, three of which change arguments rather than facts.
    38	
    39	1. **GAP-3 closed. ADR-0001 §(a)'s premise is confirmed** (E-33). `sk_A` is a witness component of the send relation; balance linkage is proved as `B₂ − b·G = sk_A·B₁` precisely because the historical randomness is unavailable to the prover; 2-special soundness extracts `sk_A`. Source: `cmpt-compact-sigma.pdf` §2.3, §2.4 Eq. 1, Thm 4.2, Thm 4.4 — Dore & Cenk, RippleX Research, 2026-05-12.
    40	2. **E-34 — nonce reuse leaks `sk_A` and is undetectable from the proofs.** Compact form transmits `(e, z…)` and not the first-round commitments, so two proofs sharing nonce material verify normally while permitting witness extraction by anyone holding both. This is **new** and it is the sharpest cryptographic constraint on ADR-0001's local-proving decision. It needs only a bug in our own signer — no adversary with local measurement, no failing transaction, no observable error.
    41	3. **The cost basis for rejecting Option 2 has materially changed.** Serialisation shipped upstream (`ripple-binary-codec@2.9.0`, E-30) and a WASM proof-generation build exists in mpt-crypto's CI (E-31). Rev 3 records this and explicitly declines to re-decide it. **Item D exists because of this.**
    42	4. **Measured performance replaced the unusable spec figures** (E-24). Stated in full, because P-8 forbids a benchmark number without its hardware, parameter set and methodology — and a packet written for reviewers who cannot open the file cannot discharge that by citing the file:
    43	
    44	   > Apple M3 Pro, macOS 26.6.1, Apple clang 21, `-O3 -DNDEBUG`, n = 50.
    45	   > Aggregated Bulletproof (m=2): proving median **34.583 ms**, verification median **16.100 ms**.
    46	   > Compact sigma, standard: prove **0.214 ms**, verify **0.328 ms**. Full Send bundle ≈ 34.8 ms prove / 16.4 ms verify.
    47	   > Spread per run in `benchmarks-2026-08-16.md`.
    48	
    49	   **Carry the block, not the numbers.** XLS-0096 §14.2's own "laptop-class CPU" figures remain unquotable (B-1).
    50	5. **The decryption "bound" is not a bound** (F-7). It is a caller-supplied window; cost is linear in window *width* with no early exit. Consequence: an application must track expected balances off-ledger. R-5 was un-retracted on this basis.
    51	6. **XLS-66 was investigated and found non-composable with confidential balances** — item E. Evidence in §3 below.

    32	~~*"A tier that reads public state and holds no credential grants no authority that did not already exist — so nothing moves, so the gate does not fire."*~~ **STRUCK.** All four legs of `REVIEW-036` ruled the claim **unsound as stated**:
    33	
    34	- ⚠ **"reads public state" is false.** `Request::Status` returns the randomness ledger's entry count, and `signer/src/store.rs:753-756` commits a record *"on success **and** on any local failure before emission — a cancelled confirmation, a failed proof, an aborted attempt."* **Public ledger history cannot reveal a declined confirmation.**
    35	- ⚠ **"holds no credential" is true and is not the property the argument needs.** It is not the same as *conveys nothing a credential produces*: the tier serves `decrypted` confidential balances out of a file written `mode 0600`, unauthenticated, over a loopback listener **that is not uid-scoped** (E-228).
    36	- ⚠ **The inference is invalid.** A trust boundary also governs confidentiality, transport exposure and **presentation integrity** — and E-209 is this project's own proof of the last, where the tier rendered a Docker-hosted Gitea's `303` as its own API response.
    37	
    38	**What is true, and it is what the milestone actually needed:** ⚠ **the tier adds no SIGNING authority.** It cannot construct `Pair` or `Prepare`, holds no key, and cannot reach the holder's confirmation — verified by both repo-access legs and by `check-web-readonly` parts 1-3. **`ADR-0003` proved that, and then wrote it as a universal about *authority*, which its evidence does not carry.**
    39	
   135	1. **Scope it as its own architecture step** with its own falsifier, and write the ADR
   136	   for the hosted surface. The questions it has to answer:
   137	   - **What does the hosted tier serve that a public node does not?** Two things are

   139	     attempts that never reached the ledger) and the decrypted issuer snapshot

   141	Fetched from `XRPLF/XRPL-Standards`, `XLS-0096-confidential-mpt/README.md`, 2026-08-24, 86,456 bytes. **The specification text is not committed to this repository**; this is the primary source for Q1.
   142	
   143	```
   144	### 10.3. Fields
   145	
   146	| Field Name               | Required? | JSON Type | Internal Type | Description |
   147	| `TransactionType`        | Yes       | string    | UINT16        | Identifies this as a ConfidentialMPTConvertBack, which is 87. |
   148	| `Account`                | Yes       | string    | ACCOUNTID     | The account performing the conversion. |
   149	| `MPTokenIssuanceID`      | Yes       | string    | UINT192       | The unique identifier for the MPT issuance. |
   150	| `MPTAmount`              | Yes       | number    | UINT64        | The plaintext amount to credit to the public balance. |
   151	| `HolderEncryptedAmount`  | Yes       | string    | BLOB          | A 66-byte ciphertext to be subtracted from the holder's sfConfidentialBalanceSpending. |
   152	| `IssuerEncryptedAmount`  | Yes       | string    | BLOB          | A 66-byte ciphertext to be subtracted from the issuer's mirror balance. |
   153	| `BlindingFactor`         | Yes       | string    | UINT256       | The 32-byte scalar value used to encrypt the amount. Used by validators to verify the ciphertexts match the plaintext MPTAmount. |
   154	| `AuditorEncryptedAmount` | No        | string    | BLOB          | A 66-byte ciphertext for the auditor. **Required** if sfAuditorEncryptionKey is present on the issuance. |
   155	| `BalanceCommitment`      | Yes       | string    | BLOB          | A 33-byte cryptographic commitment to the user's confidential spending balance. |
   156	| `ZKProof`                | Yes       | string    | BLOB          | An 816-byte bundle containing a compact sigma proof (128 bytes, proving balance ownership and key linkage) and a single Bulletproof range proof (688 bytes, proving the remaining balance is non-negative). |
   157	
   158	### 10.4.1. Data Verification (temMALFORMED / temBAD_* )
   159	1. The ConfidentialTransfer feature is not enabled. (temDISABLED)
   160	2. The account submitting the transaction is the Issuer. (temMALFORMED)
   161	3. The BlindingFactor is not exactly 32 bytes. (temMALFORMED)
   162	4. Ciphertext lengths or formats are invalid. (temBAD_CIPHERTEXT)
   163	5. MPTAmount is zero or greater than the maximum allowable supply. (temBAD_AMOUNT)
   164	
   165	### 10.4.2. Protocol-Level Failures
   166	1. The MPToken or MPTokenIssuance does not exist. (tecOBJECT_NOT_FOUND)
   167	2. The issuance does not have lsfMPTCanHoldConfidentialBalance set. (tecNO_PERMISSION)
   168	3. The user's MPToken is missing sfConfidentialBalanceSpending or sfHolderEncryptionKey. (tecNO_PERMISSION)
   169	4. The issuance has sfAuditorEncryptionKey set, but the transaction does not include sfAuditorEncryptedAmount. (tecNO_PERMISSION)
   170	5. The global sfConfidentialOutstandingAmount is less than the requested MPTAmount. (tecINSUFFICIENT_FUNDS)
   171	6. The BlindingFactor fails to verify the integrity of the ciphertexts. (tecBAD_PROOF)
   172	7. The ZKProof fails the compact sigma proof check. (tecBAD_PROOF)
   173	8. The ZKProof fails the Bulletproof range proof check. (tecBAD_PROOF)
   174	9. The account or issuance is frozen. (terFROZEN)
   175	
   176	### 10.5. State Changes
   177	- Public Balance: the user's sfMPTAmount is increased by MPTAmount.
   178	- Global Supply: sfConfidentialOutstandingAmount on the issuance is decreased by MPTAmount.
   179	- Spending Balance: sfConfidentialBalanceSpending is updated via homomorphic subtraction of HolderEncryptedAmount.
   180	- Issuer Mirror: sfIssuerEncryptedBalance is updated via homomorphic subtraction of IssuerEncryptedAmount.
   181	- Version: sfConfidentialBalanceVersion is incremented by 1.
   182	```
   183	
   184	**And §7.2, `ConfidentialMPTConvert`, for the contrast** — the field set ConvertBack was wrongly given:
   185	
   186	```
   187	| HolderEncryptionKey | No  | BLOB | The holder's ElGamal public key. Required if initializing (no key registered). Forbidden if key already registered. |
   188	| ZKProof             | No  | BLOB | A Schnorr Proof of Knowledge (PoK). Required only when HolderEncryptionKey is present. MUST be absent when HolderEncryptionKey is absent. |
   189	(§7.3.1 item 6: the length of sfZKProof is not exactly 64 bytes -> temMALFORMED)
   190	(no BalanceCommitment field exists on a Convert)
   191	```

   195	## 6. The vendored prover, verbatim
   196	
   197	`/tmp/wconf/mpt-crypto/src/utility/mpt_utility.cpp:770` — the function being wrapped. **This is the authority for Q2.**
   198	
   199	```c
   200	int mpt_get_convert_back_proof(
   201	    uint8_t const priv[32], uint8_t const pub[33],
   202	    uint8_t const context_hash[32], uint64_t const amount,
   203	    mpt_pedersen_proof_params const* params, uint8_t out_proof[816])
   204	{
   205	    if (!priv || !pub || !context_hash || !params || !out_proof) return -1;
   206	    if (amount > params->amount) return -1;
   207	    /* ... parse pub -> pk, params->ciphertext -> (b1,b2),
   208	           params->pedersen_commitment -> pc_b ... */
   209	    if (secp256k1_compact_convertback_prove(
   210	            ctx, out_proof, params->amount, priv, params->blinding_factor,
   211	            &pk, &b1, &b2, &pc_b, context_hash) != 1) return -1;
   212	
   213	    uint64_t const remaining_balance = params->amount - amount;
   214	    uint8_t* bp_ptr = out_proof + SECP256K1_COMPACT_CONVERTBACK_PROOF_SIZE;
   215	    size_t bp_len = kMPT_SINGLE_BULLETPROOF_SIZE;
   216	    uint8_t const* blinding_ptrs[1] = {params->blinding_factor};
   217	    return mpt_get_bulletproof_agg(&remaining_balance, blinding_ptrs, 1,
   218	                                   context_hash, bp_ptr, &bp_len);
   219	}
   220	```
   221	
   222	`mpt_pedersen_proof_params` is `{ pedersen_commitment[33]; amount; ciphertext[66]; blinding_factor[32]; }`.
   223	
   224	⚠ **Note `params->amount` is the BALANCE and the bare `amount` argument is the public withdrawal.** The two are one letter apart in the C and mean opposite things.

    12	---
    13	
    14	## Leg A — Codex (full repository access)
    15	
    16	# Codex Adversarial Review
    17	
    18	Target: branch diff against main
    19	Verdict: needs-attention
    20	
    21	No ship: the EncZero reimplementation diverges from upstream on the only rejection-sampling branch, and the key-file error path still leaks secret-adjacent path data.
    22	
    23	Findings:
    24	- [high] EncZero rejection sampling does not mirror upstream after an invalid scalar (tools/observer-harness.ts:67-68)
    25	  The first hash path matches the documented 51-byte layout, and zero/out-of-range scalars are rejected. The problem is the retry path: this code rehashes only the 32-byte digest (`r = sha256(r)`), while upstream `generate_canonical_encrypted_zero` copies the 32-byte digest into the first 32 bytes of the 51-byte buffer and hashes all 51 bytes again. That branch is rare for secp256k1, but this is consensus-relevant deterministic encoding; if any public input lands on an invalid first scalar, this harness will compute a different EncZero than validators and can falsely fail F-1. The live-ledger positive check and perturbed-account control do not exercise this branch, so E-59 overstates the cross-check coverage.
    26	  Recommendation: Mirror upstream exactly by keeping a 51-byte hash buffer for retries, copying the invalid digest into bytes 0..31, leaving bytes 32..50 as upstream does, and hashing 51 bytes each iteration. Add a unit test that forces the first digest invalid by stubbing the hash/scalar check so the retry branch is covered.
    27	- [medium] Missing or unreadable key files leak the key-file path through uncaught fs errors (tools/issuer-decrypt-balance.ts:51-60)
    28	  `readFileSync(args[i + 1], "utf8")` is outside a try/catch. If `--key-file` is missing, mistyped, unreadable, or points at a path the process cannot access, Bun/Node will print an uncaught exception that includes the path. That violates the stated requirement that the key-file path is not logged and creates avoidable leakage around secret storage layout.
    29	  Recommendation: Validate that `--key-file` has a non-flag value, catch filesystem errors, and emit a generic message such as `could not read key file` without including the path or raw exception text.
    30	- [medium] Issuer CLI can silently treat unknown flag values as positional accounts (tools/issuer-decrypt-balance.ts:65-68)
    31	  The positional parser removes values for only three known flags and never rejects unknown flags. With a typo or future flag like `--keyfile ./issuer.sk <account> <id>` while `WCONF_ELGAMAL_SK` is set, `./issuer.sk` becomes `account` and the real account becomes `issuanceId`; with an unknown flag whose value is a valid account, the tool can query and report on the wrong holder. For a research evidence command, silent argument reinterpretation can produce false evidence rather than a clean usage failure.
    32	  Recommendation: Replace the filter-based parser with explicit token consumption: reject unknown flags, require values for valued flags, reject extra positionals, and validate account/issuance shapes before making ledger RPCs.
    33	
    34	Next steps:
    35	- Fix the EncZero retry branch before relying on E-59 as evidence.
    36	- Harden issuer-view argument and key-file error handling, then rerun the stated negative controls.
    37
